\chapter{Introduction}\label{introduction}

Dragon Warrior IV replaces the single continuous opening of the earlier
games with four smaller adventures and a fifth chapter that brings their
heroes together. Those opening chapters are not disposable prologues:
they establish the permanent cast, determine the levels and equipment
each character brings into Chapter 5, and introduce places that the
united party will revisit later.

\section{Story So Far}\label{story-so-far}

Dragon Warrior IV begins a new continuity unconnected to the Erdrick
trilogy of the first three games. Its different world establishes the
Zenithian legend, where scattered kingdoms face a threat they do not yet
understand and rumors point toward a Hero named in prophecy.

The first four chapters follow the people whose lives will eventually
converge around that Hero. Ragnar, a royal soldier of Burland,
investigates the disappearance of local children. Princess Alena escapes
the restrictions of Santeem Castle to test her strength, accompanied by
Cristo and Brey. Taloon leaves his family in Lakanaba to pursue his
dream of owning a shop and becoming the world's greatest merchant. In
Monbaraba, the sisters Nara and Mara begin a search for the man
responsible for their father's death.

Chapter 5 begins with the Hero and turns those separate journeys into
one expedition. The earlier protagonists must be found again, the wagon
filled, the Zenithian equipment assembled, and the force behind the
spreading evil confronted. The guide will preserve each chapter's
perspective as it happens rather than revealing later connections before
the route reaches them.

\section{Chapter and Party Overview}\label{chapter-and-party-overview}

Each opening chapter has its own party, economy, and immediate
objective. Permanent characters keep their levels and carried equipment
when they return in Chapter 5, but loose gold from the solo chapters
does not carry forward. Before ending Chapters 1-4, spend surplus gold
on useful equipment or items that can be retained and sold later rather
than leaving it unused.

1 & Ragnar, with Healie as a temporary companion & Build Ragnar into a

2 & Alena / Cristo / Brey & Develop a fast physical attacker, a healer,
and an offensive-support mage while traveling from Santeem to the Endor

3 & Taloon, with optional hired help & Build Taloon's fortune, secure
his shop, and fund the tunnel that links Endor and Branca. \\
4 & Nara / Mara, with Orin as a temporary companion & Develop a support
healer and offensive mage while pursuing Balzack from Monbaraba to

Once the permanent cast is reunited, do not treat Chapter 5 as a
four-character campaign. The standard party can receive the best
equipment first, but the restricted Necrosaro achievements require
viable alternatives to the Hero. Keep the non-Hero front line and
healers equipped well enough to function when called out of the wagon.

Hero & Directly controlled physical attacker, emergency healer, and

Ragnar & High-HP, high-strength front-line fighter & Durable physical

Alena & Fast physical attacker with frequent critical hits & Strong boss
damage and one of the best answers to King Metal encounters. \\
Cristo & Durable healing and defensive support & Important sustained
healing for the main route and restricted final-party attempts. \\
Brey & Ice offense, utility magic, and Bikill support & Can accelerate

Taloon & Physical fighter with unpredictable special actions & A durable
reserve whose free-form actions can help or hinder depending on the
fight. \\
Nara & Healing and defensive support & Gives the no-Hero roster another
source of recovery, though her lower durability requires protection. \\
Mara & Fire offense and BeDragon & Provides reliable magical damage and
a tactics-dependent answer to metal enemies. \\
\end{longtable}
}

Temporary companions such as Healie, Orin, Hector, Lucia, and Doran
support particular route segments but do not replace development of the
eight permanent characters. Their arrival and departure points will be
called out where they occur.

\section{Chapter 5 Tactics and AI}\label{chapter-5-tactics-and-ai}

In Chapter 5, the player directly commands only the Hero. Other
permanent party members choose their actions according to the selected
tactic and the battle state at the moment their turn begins. They react
to damage, healing, and defeated targets caused by earlier allies, but
they do not plan around the Hero's queued action. Watch what the party
has already done before committing the Hero to healing or targeting an
enemy that may be defeated first.

The party also maintains hidden knowledge of each monster's spell
resistances. A newly encountered monster begins at knowledge rank 0.
Completing rounds against it advances that entry toward rank 3, where
the AI has exact resistance knowledge for the rest of the game. Six
casts of Ironize cover eighteen rounds and give a 99 percent chance of
reaching rank 3 from rank 0. This is excessive for ordinary encounters,
but it is a useful training method when a dangerous boss or recurring
enemy causes allies to waste turns on resisted spells. Knowledge gained
during a failed boss attempt is retained after revival.

\begin{guidetable}

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2873}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.7127}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
\color{GuideGreen}\bfseries Tactic
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
\color{GuideGreen}\bfseries Practical Use
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
Normal & Balanced default for unfamiliar encounters and most routine
travel. \\
Offensive & Prioritizes ending the fight and spends MP more freely; it
also encourages Mara to use BeDragon against metal enemies. \\
Defensive & Makes healing and defensive actions more attractive. It also
enables Nara to cast Farewell, so use it carefully when her MP is low
and allies have fallen. \\
Save MP & Reduces expensive casting and prevents Healus and BeDragon,
which can be dangerous when full-party recovery is needed. \\
Use No MP & Restricts allies to attacks and combat-usable items; useful
when conserving the last of the party's MP. \\
Try Out & Produces deliberately unpredictable actions and permits
certain unusual combat items. It is not an AI-training mode. \\
\end{longtable}
}

\end{guidetable}

For major bosses, party composition, tactic choice, learned resistance
knowledge, and the Hero's inventory matter as much as raw levels. Later
boss preparations will specify who should be active, which tactic to
begin with, when the Hero must cover healing, and whether an AI-learning
setup is worthwhile.

\section{Game-Long Achievement Plan}\label{game-long-achievement-plan}

Most achievements are earned within one location or story event. The
following achievements either span chapters, accumulate over much of
Chapter 5, or become substantially harder if preparation is postponed
until the final battle.

\begin{guidetable}

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2947}}
  >{\raggedleft\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.0830}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.6223}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
\color{GuideGreen}\bfseries Achievement
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedleft
\color{GuideGreen}\bfseries Points
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
\color{GuideGreen}\bfseries Long-Range Plan
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
Tunnel Rat & 5 & Taloon funds the Endor-Branca tunnel during Chapter 3.
Return to and use the completed tunnel in Chapter 5 so the delayed
payoff is not skipped. \\
Small Medal Collector & 50 & Track every medal from the first Chapter 5
visit to Konenber onward. Collect both Konenber ship medals before
completing the Great Lighthouse because one becomes permanently
unavailable afterward. Every later section will maintain a running total
toward the required 32. \\
High Roller & 5 & Defer the serious casino grind until Chapter 5. Build
a starting coin reserve, use the Endor poker table and double-or-nothing
game, and bank progress with normal in-game saves. Hold at least 10,000
coins at once before buying prizes. \\
The One That Didn't Get Away & 5 & Keep Alena and Mara usable for
late-game King Metal hunting. Alena's critical hits and Mara's
tactics-driven BeDragon provide better routes than ordinary attacks
alone. \\
We Don't Need Another Hero & 25 & From the Chapter 5 reunion onward,
maintain a non-Hero front line and at least one capable non-Hero healer.
The Hero's direct commands will be unavailable for this Necrosaro clear,
making tactics and equipment depth essential. \\
Just the Two of Us & 25 & Prepare a literal two-member endgame team
instead of relying only on the standard four. Preserve strong defensive
equipment and recovery options, and keep an in-game save before
Necrosaro for a dedicated attempt. \\
\end{longtable}
}

\end{guidetable}

Treat the two restricted Necrosaro achievements as separate clears when
planning. A two-person party without the Hero appears to satisfy both
written descriptions, but this guide will not depend on both unlocking
from one victory. The endgame section will provide a standard clear, a
no-Hero composition, and a two-member composition so each condition can
be attempted from the same pre-battle save.

The other achievements need attention at their specific locations rather
than continuous grinding. Hidden-treasure achievements will have exact
local checklists, chapter achievements will be confirmed before the
chapter closes, and one-time interactions will be flagged before the
route can advance past them.

\section{Achievement Routing
Overview}\label{achievement-routing-overview}

Achievements are placed in the earliest section where they can be earned
without disrupting later preparation. Each walkthrough section begins
with an achievement table listing points, the exact trigger, and route
notes. Story achievements appear at the event that completes them;
treasure achievements appear beside their final required pickup; delayed
achievements receive setup notes both when preparation begins and when
the unlock becomes available.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Achievement Alert: Achievement Name} marks the paragraph where
  an achievement should unlock.
\item
  \textbf{Achievement Setup: Achievement Name (deferred)} marks an
  action that prepares an achievement earned in a later section.
\item
  Small Medal entries include a running total and identify any
  collection that becomes unavailable after story progress.
\item
  Hidden-treasure checklists remain separate from ordinary loot so all
  required pickups can be verified before leaving the location.
\item
  Final-boss challenge achievements use explicit active-party,
  equipment, tactic, and save-point instructions rather than assuming
  the normal story party will satisfy them.
\end{itemize}

\section{How to Use This Guide}\label{how-to-use-this-guide}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Follow Chapters 1-4 in order. Their routes are self-contained, but
  their characters and carried items return in Chapter 5.
\item
  Read each section's achievement table before advancing its story
  objective. Several achievements depend on optional recruits,
  interactions, or treasures that are easy to pass.
\item
  Complete every \textbf{Achievement Alert} before crossing the
  section's departure checkpoint. Follow every deferred setup note even
  though the achievement itself will be repeated at its eventual unlock
  point.
\item
  Search drawers, jars, conspicuous ground tiles, and dead ends.
  Ordinary pickups and Small Medals are often outside treasure chests.
\item
  Before ending a solo chapter, convert spare gold into useful equipment
  or sellable items because loose gold will not join the Chapter 5
  treasury.
\item
  Treat tactic recommendations as starting settings, not guarantees. The
  AI's choices change with party health, enemy knowledge, equipment, and
  the current battle state.
\item
  Shop recommendations use four tiers: \textbf{Required}, \textbf{High},
  \textbf{Situational}, and \textbf{Skip}.
\item
  Boss preparation uses the same fields throughout: level, active party,
  wagon reserves, equipment, consumables, required spells, tactic,
  recovery point, and opening plan.
\item
  Use each section's return link to jump back to the table of contents
  before moving to another route segment.
\end{itemize}

\hyperref[table-of-contents]{Return to Table of Contents}

\chapter{Chapter 1: Ragnar and the Missing
Children}\label{chapter-1-ragnar-and-the-missing-children}

\section{Section Achievements: Chapter
1}\label{section-achievements-chapter-1}

\begin{guidetable}

Reunited & 5 & Help Flora find her missing husband. & Identify Alex in

Chapter 1 follows Ragnar, a soldier of Burland ordered to investigate
why children have been disappearing across the kingdom. Ragnar is
physically sturdy but slow, has no MP or spells, and fights alone until
he finds an optional companion. Equipment and Medical Herb management
therefore matter more than complicated battle commands: Ragnar can
attack, parry, use an item, or run, but he cannot repair a bad situation
with magic on his own.

The chapter's critical path is:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Investigate Burland and learn that Flora's husband Alex is missing.
\item
  Cross the Cave to Izmit and question the villagers by day and night.
\item
  Identify the prisoner as Alex, bring Flora to him, and learn the
  location of the Secret Playground.
\item
  Explore the hidden well, recruit Healie, and recover the Flying Shoes.
\item
  Use the Flying Shoes beside Loch Tower, rescue the children, and
  defeat Saro's Shadow.
\item
  Return the children to Izmit, convert spare gold into carried value,
  and report to the King of Burland.
\end{enumerate}

\section{Ragnar's Starting Position}\label{ragnars-starting-position}

\begin{guidetable}

\end{guidetable}

Ragnar has high Strength and exceptionally high HP growth, but very low
Agility. He usually acts after enemies and gains little natural Defense
from Agility, so preventing damage through shields and armor is more
reliable than buying every intermediate weapon. His chapter-end gear and
carried inventory return with him in Chapter 5.

\section{Burland Castle and Town}\label{burland-castle-and-town}

After the opening audience, Ragnar begins in Burland Castle's throne
room. The King orders the royal soldiers to discover who is taking the
kingdom's children. Before leaving, speak with the people in the castle
for the local context, then go to the southeast room on 1F. Search the
row of dressers and take the \textbf{Medical Herb from the second
dresser from the right}. It is hidden rather than displayed as a chest.

Leave the castle for the attached town. Flora stands near the southwest
side of town and explains that her husband, Alex, disappeared while
looking into the same mystery. Speaking with her now establishes the
lead needed later, although Ragnar cannot resolve it until he identifies
Alex in Izmit.

The House of Healing near the town entrance is the save point. Its
priest can also cure poison, curses, and a defeated character. Make a
save before the first field trip. The inn charges 4G, but at the opening
budget it is better to take short loops outside and use herbs only when
the experience and gold gained justify the cost.

The later Izmit shop carries the two defensive upgrades that matter
most: Half Plate Armor and Iron Shield.

\subsection{Opening Training Loop}\label{opening-training-loop}

Fight near Burland until Ragnar reaches about level 3. Stay close enough
to retreat before a bad group can turn into a defeat. Slimes and Stag
Beetles are harmless training targets; Giant Worms and Diverats are
still manageable but have enough HP to extend a fight. Babbles are the
main logistical threat because poison persists after battle. At night,
either carry an Antidote Herb or avoid stretching the trip beyond a safe
return.

Buy the Leather Shield once Ragnar has 90G, then rest, save, and leave
Burland through the northwest route toward the river cave. The exact
world-map approach bends west and north around the river; enter the cave
that crosses to Izmit's side rather than trying to follow the shoreline
directly.

\section{Cave to Izmit}\label{cave-to-izmit}

\textbf{Recommended level:} 3\\
\textbf{Recovery:} Burland before entry; Izmit after exiting\\
\textbf{Fixed treasure:} Medical Herb, 40G

This is a short one-floor passage and the first chance to learn the
guide's dungeon discipline: take side branches when they appear, account
for every fixed pickup, and leave before low HP forces an expensive
recovery.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  From the entrance, follow the passage north and turn right at the
  first available branch.
\item
  Enter the dark passage to the east. It ends at a chest containing a
  \textbf{Medical Herb}.
\item
  Backtrack to the main route and continue north into the room with a
  central pond. The soldier here is lost and does not advance the story.
\item
  Walk clockwise around the water and take the passage on the left.
\item
  Follow it north to the chest containing \textbf{40G}.
\item
  Continue north to the exit stairs.
\end{enumerate}

Outside, travel east; Izmit lies a short distance to the north. Healers
can prolong cave battles by repeatedly casting Heal. Focus on one target
until it falls rather than spreading damage and giving it time to
recover.

Enemies}\label{burland-and-izmit-region-enemies}

\section{Izmit Investigation and the
Reunion}\label{izmit-investigation-and-the-reunion}

Izmit's item shop and inn are near the entrance; its weapon and armor
shops are farther north. Search the three dressers in the room on the
right side of the inn for a hidden \textbf{Medical Herb}. Talk to the
adults and children during the day, including the schoolchildren, then
inspect the jail below the stairs at the north end of town. The prisoner
cannot initially explain who he is.

Stay at the inn or walk outside until night. Return to the schoolyard
and speak to the boy moving around the playground. He identifies the
prisoner as Alex. Go back underground and speak to the prisoner again so
Ragnar can confirm the lead.

Return through the cave to Burland and talk to Flora. She will follow
Ragnar without occupying his battle slot. Escort her back through the
Cave to Izmit and take her directly to Alex's cell. Flora restores his
memory, and Alex explains that the children visit a hidden playground in
the forest: from the sign outside Izmit, walk \textbf{four steps south
and four steps east}.

\begin{achievementalert}

\textbf{Achievement Alert: Reunited}

\textbf{Trigger:} Complete the conversation in Alex's cell after
bringing Flora from Burland. The achievement description is ``Help Flora
find her missing husband.''

\end{achievementalert}

This event is the progression gate for the Secret Playground. Rest and
save in Izmit before following Alex's directions.

The ideal end-of-chapter set is \textbf{Sword of Malice / Half Plate
Armor / Iron Shield / Wooden Hat}. It is not necessary to own all three
armor upgrades before entering the tower, but every permanent defensive
purchase reduces healing pressure and remains useful later.

\section{Secret Playground}\label{secret-playground}

\textbf{Recommended level:} 6\\
\textbf{Recovery:} Izmit before and after the trip\\
\textbf{Fixed treasure:} 2 Medical Herbs, 600G, Agility Seed, Flying
Shoes\\
\textbf{Optional recruit:} Healie

Walk to the sign outside Izmit, count four tiles south and four tiles
east, and enter the hidden forest clearing. Walk north and descend the
well. A voice gives directions through the passages. Following it leads
safely toward the objective and suppresses most encounters; leaving its
route leads to the optional treasure and Healie. This guide deliberately
leaves the safe path to clear the area.

\subsection{B1: First Branches}\label{b1-first-branches}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Follow the initial passage south.
\item
  At the first split, continue south as the voice directs.
\item
  At the T intersection, go west.
\item
  At the next split, go south and ignore the staircase to the north for
  now.
\item
  Continue until the voice speaks again. Ignore it and go east into the
  side room.
\item
  Follow the winding passage to its dead end and open the chest for a
  \textbf{Medical Herb}.
\item
  Return to the last intersection, go south, and descend to B2.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{B2: Recruit Healie Before Taking the
Shoes}\label{b2-recruit-healie-before-taking-the-shoes}

From the stairs, walk east. When the voice tells Ragnar to turn, ignore
it and continue all the way east to the dead end. Speak to the Healer
slime there and answer \textbf{Yes} when he offers to join.

\begin{achievementalert}

\textbf{Achievement Alert: Making Friends}

\textbf{Trigger:} Accept Healie into the party. The achievement
description is ``Let the Healer slime join your party.''

\end{achievementalert}

Healie is optional for story completion but central to the safe route.
He acts independently, has a basic physical attack, and casts Heal on
Ragnar during battle. He also gives enemies a second target. Ragnar
still needs Medical Herbs because Healie can spend all of his MP or wait
longer than desired to heal.

Retrace the passage to the voice, then take the northern route. Continue
north through the next intersection. At the following T split, go east
against the voice's direction and follow the long passage to the chest
containing \textbf{600G}. This money should fund most or all of the Iron
Shield, depending on earlier spending.

Return to that T split and take the western branch recommended by the
voice. Open the chest on the platform for the \textbf{Flying Shoes}.
This key item is required to enter Loch Tower.

\subsection{Exit Route and Hidden
Pots}\label{exit-route-and-hidden-pots}

Return to the stairs in the southwest corner of B2 and climb to B1. Go
north, west, north, then east to reach the staircase that was bypassed
earlier. Follow the zigzag passage toward the entrance. Near the first
split, take the western branch into the room with cracked flooring and
deliberately fall through the opening.

After landing, climb the nearby stairs into the small room with five
pots. Search all five to collect a \textbf{Medical Herb} and an
\textbf{Agility Seed}, then use the eastern staircase to emerge near the
well. Walk southeast around the trees and leave the clearing to the
west.

Do not spend the Agility Seed yet. Ragnar's low Agility makes him an
intuitive target, but the seed remains in his carried inventory and can
be allocated after the full Chapter 5 roster is available. The supplied
walkthrough recommends saving early stat seeds for that decision point.

\subsection{Secret Playground Enemies}\label{secret-playground-enemies}

\end{guidetable}

Return to Izmit, rest, save, and prepare for the chapter's only major
dungeon. Aim for level 8 or 9. Buy the Iron Shield and Wooden Hat if
possible, then Half Plate Armor as funds permit. A Chain Sickle is the
only worthwhile temporary weapon purchase, and even it is optional
because Loch Tower contains the Sword of Malice. Carry at least three
Medical Herbs on Ragnar; four provides a safer buffer if Healie spends
his MP before the boss.

\section{Loch Tower}\label{loch-tower}

\textbf{Recommended level:} 8-9 on entry; about 9-10 for the boss\\
\textbf{Required item:} Flying Shoes\\
\textbf{Recovery:} Unlimited full HP/MP restoration on the basement
recovery tile\\
\textbf{Fixed treasure:} Wing of Wyvern, Strength Seed, 640G, Scale
Shield, Luck Seed, Sword of Malice

Walk west from Izmit to the water surrounding Loch Tower. Stand beside
the moat and \textbf{use} the Flying Shoes from Ragnar's inventory.
Ragnar and Healie land on the tower's roof, where they see a child being
led below. The supplied guides count the roof as either the entry level
or 4F, so this route identifies floors by sequence and landmark as well
as number.

\subsection{Roof to 2F}\label{roof-to-2f}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Descend from the roof and enter the northern passage.
\item
  Take the left branch to the dead-end chest containing a \textbf{Wing
  of Wyvern}.
\item
  Return to the entry stairs, take the southern route, then walk east
  past the soldier.
\item
  Descend the stairs at the end to reach 2F.
\item
  From the 2F stairs, walk north past the large door and climb the
  northeast stairs.
\item
  On the upper landing, open the nearby chest for a \textbf{Strength
  Seed}, then return to 2F.
\item
  Enter the large room on 2F and open the chest for \textbf{640G}.
\item
  Continue clockwise around the floor. At the pair of large pillars,
  walk north between them into the covered room and take the
  \textbf{Scale Shield}.
\item
  Continue north and descend to 1F.
\end{enumerate}

Equip the Scale Shield only if Ragnar still has the Leather Shield. If
he already bought the superior Iron Shield, keep the Scale Shield as
carried value for Chapter 5 rather than downgrading.

\subsection{1F Treasure and Recovery
Hub}\label{f-treasure-and-recovery-hub}

From the 1F stairs, first go south and descend to the basement. Step
onto the white tile at the north end to restore Ragnar and Healie to
full HP and MP. The tile can be used repeatedly, making this the best
place in the chapter to finish any required leveling.

Return to 1F and open the large door north of the stairs. The eastern
branch leads to a chest containing a \textbf{Luck Seed}. The mirrored
western branch leads to the \textbf{Sword of Malice}. Equip it
immediately: its +45 attack is 17 points stronger than the Iron Spear
and 25 stronger than the Chain Sickle. When used as an item in battle,
it produces a group-targeting Firebal effect, making it useful against
groups or enemies whose Defense has been raised by Upper.

Keep both seeds unused for Chapter 5 allocation. Heal again after
collecting the weapon if the detours cost meaningful HP or MP.

\subsection{Loch Tower Enemies}\label{loch-tower-enemies}

\end{guidetable}

Once Ragnar has the Sword of Malice, most ordinary enemies should fall
in one or two attacks. Use the basement tile instead of consuming herbs
during the final preparation loop. Finish at level 9 or 10, refill
Healie's MP, place three or four Medical Herbs in Ragnar's inventory,
and save in Izmit if you want a protected attempt before committing to
the boss route.

\subsection{Route to the Children}\label{route-to-the-children}

From the 1F stairs leading to the recovery basement, take the west
passage. Follow it south and then east across the bottom of the floor.
Pass the tower's ground-level front entrance and continue east to the
wall, then turn north and descend the stairs. Follow the lower passage
east and enter the northern room. A child warns Ragnar before the
confrontation. Heal before stepping onto the platform and speaking to
the monster.

\section{Boss: Saro's Shadow and Giant
Eyeball}\label{boss-saros-shadow-and-giant-eyeball}

\begin{guidetable}

Level & 9-10 \\
Party & Ragnar / Healie \\

\end{guidetable}

The encounter pays a combined \textbf{100 EXP and 100G}. Attack the
Giant Eyeball first and finish it cleanly; its low-HP change makes
leaving it wounded more dangerous than removing it. Once it falls,
attack Saro's Shadow every round while Healie handles routine healing.

Do not assume Healie will always heal at Ragnar's preferred threshold.
If Ragnar falls into danger, use a Medical Herb rather than gambling on
Healie's next action. If Healie runs out of MP, Ragnar must alternate
between attacking and self-healing. There is no reason to conserve herbs
past survival: the chapter is almost over, while defeat costs half of
Ragnar's current gold and requires another tower descent.

The Sword of Malice's item effect is less valuable here than a normal
physical attack against a single target. Use the weapon as a weapon,
keep Ragnar healthy enough to survive Saro's Shadow's next action, and
grind down the boss's 250 HP. The battle ends as soon as both enemies
are defeated; any listed monster drop remains chance-based and is not
required for progression.

\section{Returning the Children}\label{returning-the-children}

After the battle, the children follow Ragnar. Backtrack to the tower's
ground-level front entrance and step outside. The moat prevents a direct
walk to Izmit. If Ragnar still has the Wing of Wyvern, use it to return
immediately. Otherwise, use the Flying Shoes outside the tower to return
to the roof, then walk off the south edge; Ragnar, Healie, and the
children land safely beyond the moat.

Enter Izmit to reunite the children with their families. Random
encounters cease for the return to Burland, so this is the chapter's
final preparation window rather than another combat leg.

Before speaking to the King, spend nearly all remaining gold. Loose gold
does not carry into Chapter 5, while Ragnar's equipped gear and carried
items do. Prioritize the permanent set in this order:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Half Plate Armor if Ragnar does not own it.
\item
  Iron Shield if Ragnar does not own it.
\item
  Wooden Hat if Ragnar does not own it.
\item
  Extra equipment or consumables that retain resale or practical value
  in Chapter 5.
\end{enumerate}

Keep the Sword of Malice equipped and retain the Strength, Agility, and
Luck Seeds. Do not sell unique or useful carried items merely to reach
exactly zero gold. The objective is to avoid abandoning currency, not to
weaken Ragnar's returning inventory.

Leave Izmit, cross the connecting cave back to Burland, enter the
castle, and approach the throne. Ragnar reports what the kidnappers were
seeking and declares his intention to find and protect the Hero. The
King awards Ragnar \textbf{3,000 EXP}, normally raising him to
approximately level 12 or 13, and Chapter 1 ends.

\begin{achievementalert}

\textbf{Achievement Alert: In Search of a Hero}

\textbf{Trigger:} Finish the audience with the King of Burland and
complete Chapter 1.

\end{achievementalert}

\section{Chapter 1 Completion Check}\label{chapter-1-completion-check}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Achievements:} Reunited, Making Friends, and In Search of a
  Hero unlocked.
\item
  \textbf{Ragnar:} Approximately level 12-13 after the King's 3,000 EXP
  reward.
\item
  \textbf{Preferred equipment:} Sword of Malice / Half Plate Armor /
  Iron Shield / Wooden Hat.
\item
  \textbf{Carried forward:} Ragnar's level, equipment, inventory, and
  unused stat seeds.
\item
  \textbf{Not carried forward:} Loose Chapter 1 gold.
\item
  \textbf{Temporary companion:} Healie leaves when the chapter ends.
\item
  \textbf{Missable check:} Healie was recruited before taking the
  children home.
\item
  \textbf{Next chapter:} Princess Alena's adventure begins in Santeem
  Castle.
\end{itemize}

\hyperref[table-of-contents]{Return to Table of Contents}

\chapter{Chapter 2: Alena's Journey to
Endor}\label{chapter-2-alenas-journey-to-endor}

\section{Section Achievements: Chapter
2}\label{section-achievements-chapter-2}

\begin{guidetable}

Sneaking Out & 5 & Find a way out of the Castle. & Complete the dialogue
gate in Santeem, break through Alena's repaired bedroom wall, and leave
the castle grounds. \\
The Offering & 5 & Bring peace to Tempe. & Volunteer as the sacrifice
and defeat the Chameleon Humanoid with its two Rabidhounds. \\
The Kidnapped Imposter & 5 & Rescue the fake princess. & Exchange the
Golden Bracelet for May at Frenor's graveyard at night. \\
Speak No Evil & 5 & Restore the King's Voice. & Recover the Birdsong
Nectar and use it while facing the King of Santeem. \\
The Empty Castle & 10 & Complete Chapter 2. & Win the Endor Tournament,
investigate the deserted Santeem Castle, and leave through its front
entrance. \\

\end{guidetable}

Chapter 2 follows Princess Alena of Santeem, whose desire to see the
world is stronger than her father's order to remain inside the castle.
Unlike Ragnar's solo opening, this chapter quickly forms a balanced
three-person party: Alena supplies fast physical damage, Cristo supplies
healing and defensive magic, and Brey supplies attack magic and enemy
debuffs.

The chapter's critical path is:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Break out of Santeem Castle and meet Cristo and Brey outside.
\item
  Equip and train the party around Surene before traveling to Tempe.
\item
  Volunteer as Tempe's offering and defeat the monsters threatening the
  village.
\item
  Investigate the false princess in Frenor, recover the Golden Bracelet,
  and exchange it for May and the Thief's Key.
\item
  Visit the Bazaar, answer Santeem's summons, and trace the cure for the
  King's lost voice to Birdsong Tower.
\item
  Restore the King's voice, cross the travel-door shrine to Endor, and
  prepare Alena for the solo tournament.
\item
  Win five consecutive matches, return to the now-empty Santeem Castle,
  and leave to close the chapter.
\end{enumerate}

\section{Chapter 2 Party Development
Plan}\label{chapter-2-party-development-plan}

\begin{guidetable}

Alena & Fast physical attacker and eventual solo tournament fighter & No

All three permanent characters retain their levels and personal
equipment when they return in Chapter 5. Loose Chapter 2 gold does not
carry forward. Casino coins are tracked separately and remain available
later, but this route still defers the serious casino grind until
Chapter 5. The final Endor visit is therefore both a tournament
checkpoint and the last opportunity to convert ordinary gold into
lasting equipment.

\section{Santeem Castle: The Escape}\label{santeem-castle-the-escape}

\textbf{Starting level:} 1\\
\textbf{Fixed pickups:} Feather Hat, Fairy Water, Wing of Wyvern\\
\textbf{Recovery:} House of Healing inside the castle

The chapter opens in Santeem's throne room. After the King forbids Alena
from leaving, speak with Brey in the throne room, Cristo on 1F, the
guards at the front entrance, and the other castle residents. These
conversations advance the repair work in Alena's bedroom. If the escape
route is not ready, continue speaking with anyone you missed before
returning upstairs.

From the throne room, take the stairs to the King's right up to 3F.
Enter Alena's bedroom and speak with the repairman at the damaged wall.
Search the room's dressers for a \textbf{Feather Hat} and equip it
immediately; its +8 Defense is the best helmet Alena can use during this
chapter.

Return to 1F and enter the House of Healing where Cristo was standing.
Search the dressers in its back room for \textbf{Fairy Water}. Enter the
room in the northwest part of 1F and search the right dresser for a
\textbf{Wing of Wyvern}. After completing the castle conversations,
return to Alena's bedroom. Stand directly in front of the repaired wall,
use Search, and choose to kick it down. Walk through the opening, drop
onto the roof, and step off the north edge to land outside the castle
wall. Leave the castle grounds to the north.

\begin{achievementalert}

\textbf{Achievement Alert: Sneaking Out}

\textbf{Trigger:} Escape Santeem Castle through the repaired bedroom
wall and leave the grounds.

\end{achievementalert}

Walk west toward the nearby town. Cristo and Brey intercept Alena and
insist on joining, creating the permanent Chapter 2 party. Enter Surene
immediately rather than fighting before the two companions arrive.

\section{Surene: Equipment and Early
Levels}\label{surene-equipment-and-early-levels}

\textbf{Recommended level before leaving:} 4\\
\textbf{Inn:} 2G per person\\
\textbf{Fixed pickups:} None

Surene is the party's first safe base. Speak to everyone, including the
poet Marone on the second-floor balcony of the main building; his
information becomes a required link later. Save at the House of Healing,
then make short training loops around Surene and Santeem until the party
reaches approximately level 4.

\subsection{Surene Shops}\label{surene-shops}

High & Leather Armor & 180G & +12 defense & Useful on all three
characters; protect Brey first if he is falling in one or two hits. \\

\end{guidetable}

Use hand-me-downs instead of buying three complete sets at once. A
Copper Sword for Cristo frees his Club for Alena, and one Leather Shield
on Brey removes the party's weakest defensive point. Add Leather Armor
as gold permits. Do not remain here until every slot is filled; Tempe's
route and boss reward provide more gold and better weapon access.

Enemies}\label{santeem-and-surene-region-enemies}

When the party is near level 4, rest and save in Surene. Travel north of
Santeem Castle, follow the path east at the sign, and enter the mountain
village of Tempe.

\section{Tempe and the Offering}\label{tempe-and-the-offering}

\textbf{Recommended level:} 5 on arrival; 6 before the boss\\
\textbf{Recovery:} Inn for 3G per person; save before accepting the
offering\\
\textbf{Fixed pickup:} Lifeforce Nuts, available after the boss

Speak with the villagers to learn that monsters demand the sacrifice of
young girls. Enter the building east of the inn and speak with the
mayor; agree to help the village. The shaman in the House of Healing at
the north end can then prepare Alena as the next offering.

Do not accept until the party is ready, because the conversation moves
directly into the boss sequence. Reach level 6, refill Cristo and Brey's
MP, place at least one Medical Herb on Alena or Brey, and improve Brey's
armor. Cristo's Upper, Surround, and Heal and Brey's Sap and Icebolt are
the important tools; extra attack power on the casters is secondary.

\subsection{Tempe Shop}\label{tempe-shop}

The northwest shop opens after the boss, so its stock cannot be used for
the first attempt.

When ready, speak to the shaman, agree to serve as the offering, and
enter the chamber to the south. The party is carried to the outdoor
platform, where a Chameleon Humanoid and two Rabidhounds attack.

\subsection{Boss: Chameleon Humanoid and Two
Rabidhounds}\label{boss-chameleon-humanoid-and-two-rabidhounds}

\chaptermark{Chapter 2: Alena's Journey to Endor}

The full encounter awards \textbf{150 EXP and 100G}: 136 EXP and 60G
from the Chameleon Humanoid plus 7 EXP and 20G from each Rabidhound.
Have Cristo cast Upper on Brey first, since Brey is the easiest
character to lose. Surround reduces the hounds' physical accuracy, while
Brey's Sap accelerates whichever target the party chooses. Use Icebolt
when another debuff is unnecessary.

The supplied strategies disagree on the opening target. Removing a
Rabidhound first reduces the enemy action count; eliminating the
Chameleon Humanoid first prevents healing. Use the first round to judge
incoming damage. If Brey is under immediate pressure, remove the hounds
one at a time. If the Chameleon erases meaningful damage with Heal,
concentrate all attacks on it. Do not split damage across all three
enemies.

Cristo should heal whenever a party member cannot safely absorb the next
round. Alena attacks unless she must use the emergency herb because
Cristo is occupied or acts too late.

\begin{achievementalert}

\textbf{Achievement Alert: The Offering}

\textbf{Trigger:} Defeat the Chameleon Humanoid and both Rabidhounds,
ending Tempe's sacrifices.

\end{achievementalert}

After the victory, the northwest shop opens and Tempe's northern exit
becomes available. Return to the outdoor offering platform, stand on the
visibly cracked tile at its center, and use Search to uncover
\textbf{Lifeforce Nuts}. Save them for Chapter 5 allocation rather than
committing the permanent HP increase before the full roster is
available.

Rest, save, and leave through the House of Healing to the north. Follow
the mountain route northeast, then turn east toward the waterfront town
of Frenor.

\section{Frenor and the False
Princess}\label{frenor-and-the-false-princess}

\textbf{Recommended level:} 6-7 on arrival; 9 before the cave\\
\textbf{Recovery:} Inn for 4G per person; House of Healing in the
southwest\\
\textbf{Fixed town pickup:} Medical Herb after the ransom exchange

Speak with the townspeople to hear that the Princess of Santeem is
staying at the inn. Since Alena is the real princess, enter the inn near
the center of town and go upstairs. Speak with the occupants to trigger
the kidnapping scene, then follow the abductors through the inn's
concealed exterior route.

Return to town and speak with everyone again. The boy near the southeast
shop has the ransom demand: bring the Golden Bracelet to the northwest
graveyard at night. The bracelet is kept in the cave south of Frenor.

The inn and House of Healing become available after the kidnapping. When
entering the House of Healing, watch the priest run forward from the
rear garden and remember the square where he was standing. That tile
holds a hidden item after the rescue.

Reach about level 9 before entering the cave. Cristo learns
\textbf{Antidote at level 8}, removing much of the poison pressure, and
Brey learns \textbf{Outside at level 7}. Preserve at least 8 MP on Brey
so the party can leave immediately after finding the bracelet. Buy Chain
Mail for Alena and Cristo as funds allow, a Wooden Hat for Cristo, and
another Boomerang if basic attacks remain weak.

\subsection{Frenor Shops}\label{frenor-shops}

The weapon shop east of the central pond opens only after May is
rescued.

\section{Cave South of Frenor}\label{cave-south-of-frenor}

\textbf{Recommended level:} 9\\
\textbf{Fixed treasure:} Wing of Wyvern, 360G, Agility Seed, Magic
Potion, Golden Bracelet\\
\textbf{Exit plan:} Brey's Outside spell with at least 8 MP remaining

The upper floor is manageable, but the lower floor adds enemies that
attack groups, drain MP, poison, or reduce Defense. Conserve Cristo's MP
for healing and Antidote. Brey should use ordinary attacks against weak
groups and reserve spellcasting for dangerous combinations.

\subsection{Upper Floor}\label{upper-floor}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  From the entrance, walk southwest a few steps and take the southern
  dark passage to a chest containing a \textbf{Wing of Wyvern}.
\item
  Return to the main passage and continue west into the large room.
\item
  Ignore the central platform briefly and take the northern dark passage
  to a chest containing \textbf{360G}.
\item
  Return to the large room and climb onto the central platform for the
  \textbf{Agility Seed}.
\item
  Walk counterclockwise around the platform, turn west, then follow the
  passage north and east to the stairs.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Lower Floor}\label{lower-floor}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Walk south from the stairs and take the eastern passage.
\item
  In the large room, enter the dark western branch and open the southern
  chest for a \textbf{Magic Potion}.
\item
  Return to the large room and continue south through the passage partly
  obscured by undergrowth.
\item
  Move clockwise through the room of rocks and bones; the bones are
  walkable floor rather than barriers.
\item
  Follow the final western route and open the lone chest for the
  \textbf{Golden Bracelet}.
\end{enumerate}

Use Brey's Outside spell as soon as the bracelet is secured. The Magic
Potion can restore enough MP if the trip consumed the reserve intended
for escape.

\subsection{Frenor Region and Cave
Enemies}\label{frenor-region-and-cave-enemies}

\section{The Ransom Exchange}\label{the-ransom-exchange}

Return to Frenor and wait until night. Go to the graveyard in the
northwest corner and speak to the kidnappers. Hand over the Golden
Bracelet; May is released and gives the party the \textbf{Thief's Key}.

\begin{achievementalert}

\textbf{Achievement Alert: The Kidnapped Imposter}

\textbf{Trigger:} Complete the nighttime exchange at Frenor's graveyard
and rescue May.

\end{achievementalert}

After May leaves, revisit the House of Healing. Walk into the rear
garden and Search the exact tile where the priest stood before running
forward on the first visit. Pick up the hidden \textbf{Medical Herb}.
Speak with the townspeople again to learn about the desert Bazaar, then
visit the newly opened weapon shop if Alena still needs a Chain Sickle.

Leave Frenor and travel south past the Golden Bracelet cave. A shrine
lies to the east, but it is not the next objective. Continue southwest
into the desert and step onto the green oasis tile to enter the Bazaar.

\section{The Bazaar and Santeem's
Summons}\label{the-bazaar-and-santeems-summons}

\textbf{Recommended level:} 9-10\\
\textbf{Inn:} 6G per person\\
\textbf{Fixed pickups:} Strength Seed, Gum Pod

Explore the market and speak with the merchants. On the west side, a
Santeem guard stops Alena and reports that something is wrong with the
King. This is the story signal to return home, but collect the Bazaar's
items and evaluate its equipment first.

Enter the small house on the Bazaar's south side. Search the two pots
immediately beside the woman to find a \textbf{Strength Seed} and a
\textbf{Gum Pod}. Retain the seed for Chapter 5 allocation.

High & Venomous Dagger & 750G & +24 attack & Brey's best practical
Chapter 2 weapon and potentially useful against Metal Slimes. \\
Situational & Iron Spear & 880G & +28 attack & Good for Cristo, but save
another 370G for the Morning Star when possible. \\
High & Morning Star & 1,250G & +33 attack & Cristo's best conventional
Chapter 2 weapon. \\
\end{longtable}
}

\end{guidetable}

Cast Return to Santeem if Brey has learned it; otherwise walk back
through the northern route. Enter the castle and speak to the King on
2F. He has lost his voice, so the chancellor directs the party to the
old man behind the castle.

Go to Alena's room on 3F, pass through the broken wall, and drop to the
roof. This time, use the Thief's Key on the western outbuilding. Speak
with the old man, Goz, and search his dresser for a \textbf{Wing of
Wyvern}. Goz remembers that Marone once recovered from a damaged voice.

Enter Surene and speak with Marone on the second-floor balcony. He
identifies Birdsong Nectar as the cure and says he obtained it at the
Bazaar. Return to the Bazaar, walk behind the item merchant, and speak
to him from the rear. He no longer has the nectar and directs the party
to Birdsong Tower on the peninsula far southwest of the oasis.

\section{Birdsong Tower}\label{birdsong-tower}

\textbf{Recommended level:} 12\\
\textbf{Recovery:} Basement inn, 6G per person\\
\textbf{Required item:} Thief's Key\\
\textbf{Fixed treasure:} Strength Seed, 1,200G, Wing of Wyvern, Birdsong
Nectar

Brey should reach level 11 and learn \textbf{Snowstorm} before the
climb. The spell can erase dangerous groups, but it is expensive, so
reserve it for combinations such as multiple Butterfly Dragons or
Pteranodons. Cristo should preserve most of his MP for healing and
poison removal.

\subsection{Birdsong Tower Enemies}\label{birdsong-tower-enemies}

\end{guidetable}

Enter the central room on 1F. The stairs down lead to the basement inn,
which allows a full recovery without returning to the Bazaar. Return to
1F, circle clockwise around the floor, open the large door with the
Thief's Key, and climb the stairs beyond it.

\subsection{Treasure Detour}\label{treasure-detour}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  On 2F, walk west to the wall, then south.
\item
  Follow the bending southeast route to the stairs.
\item
  On the upper landing, walk north a few steps and then west. Continue
  north through the apparently closed end into the small treasure room.
\item
  Open both chests for a \textbf{Strength Seed} and \textbf{1,200G}.
\item
  Return to 2F and backtrack to the original stairs from 1F.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Route to the Summit}\label{route-to-the-summit}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  From the original 2F stair area, go east through the passage and
  immediately south.
\item
  Follow the path west, pass through the southern opening, then continue
  east to the stairs in the southeast corner.
\item
  On the next floor, go north through the opening and take the northwest
  chest containing a \textbf{Wing of Wyvern}.
\item
  Return toward the stairs, take the western route, then follow the
  narrow path clockwise around the open pit. Falling returns the party
  to a lower floor but does not lose treasure.
\item
  Climb the final stairs and follow the upper floor's linear path to the
  summit.
\end{enumerate}

Approach the two dancing elves. They flee and leave the \textbf{Birdsong
Nectar} in the small sandy patch where they were standing. The supplied
walkthroughs disagree about which compass corner contains it, so do not
rely on a directional label: use Search on the sand tiles until the
nectar is found. It cannot be seen as a chest.

Use the tower's Wing of Wyvern or Brey's Return spell to leave, then
return to Santeem Castle. Stand directly in front of the King and use
the Birdsong Nectar from the item menu. His voice returns, he describes
ominous dreams, and he finally permits Alena to continue traveling.

\begin{achievementalert}

\textbf{Achievement Alert: Speak No Evil}

\textbf{Trigger:} Use the Birdsong Nectar on the King of Santeem and
restore his voice.

\end{achievementalert}

\section{The Road to Endor}\label{the-road-to-endor}

Return to Frenor, travel south past the Golden Bracelet cave, and enter
the shrine that was bypassed earlier. Step onto the travel door in the
northern room. On the far side, leave the portal room and use the shrine
inn if needed. Ragnar may appear here at night, providing an optional
link to Chapter 1, but speaking with him is not required for
progression.

Leave the shrine and follow the grass route southeast through the
mountains to Endor. The enemies here are stronger than those west of the
portal, so arrive with full HP and enough MP to reach the city safely.

\subsection{Endor Region Enemies}\label{endor-region-enemies}

Metal Slimes are excellent experience but are not required for an
achievement; the set's metal-monster achievement specifically requires a
\textbf{King Metal} much later. Do not delay the tournament waiting for
a rare Metal Slime victory.

\section{Endor and Tournament
Preparation}\label{endor-and-tournament-preparation}

\textbf{Recommended level:} 12-13\\
\textbf{Town inn:} 6G per person\\
\textbf{Tournament inn:} 8G per person\\
\textbf{Fixed pickup:} Lifeforce Nuts\\
\textbf{Mandatory purchase:} Iron Claw, 1,500G

Explore Endor and speak with its residents. The casino is beneath the
inn, but the achievement route defers serious gambling until Chapter 5.
Do not spend the 1,500G reserved for Alena's Iron Claw or the money
needed for healing and armor.

\begin{achievementsetup}

\textbf{Achievement Setup: High Roller (deferred)}

\textbf{Requirement:} Hold at least 10,000 Casino coins at once. The
casino is available now, but the routed attempt occurs in Chapter 5 when
the party has a stronger economy. Any optional play here must not
endanger the Iron Claw purchase.

\end{achievementsetup}

Enter Endor Castle through the central door and speak with the Princess,
then the King. The winner of the tournament is expected to marry the
Princess; Alena can nullify that outcome by winning herself.

Leave the castle and re-enter through its western door to reach the
coliseum corridor. Before going north, enter the break in the west wall
and walk south to the basement stairs. Open the lone chest for
\textbf{Lifeforce Nuts}, then return to the corridor.

Speak to the coliseum attendant, then enter the fighter's quarters to
the left. This room has an inn, a House of Healing, and a temporary shop
selling Medical Herbs for 8G and the \textbf{Iron Claw for 1,500G}. Buy
and equip the Iron Claw before entering the tournament. It gives Alena
+38 Attack, remains in her personal equipment when she returns in
Chapter 5, and stays her best weapon well into that chapter. This is the
last guaranteed Iron Claw: the temporary shop closes after the
tournament, although Skeletons and Phantom Messengers can drop
replacement copies later.

Move the Thief's Key and all nonessential items from Alena to Cristo or
Brey. Fill Alena's remaining inventory slots with Medical Herbs, then
rest and save. Cristo and Brey do not participate in the matches, so
only Alena's equipment, HP, and inventory matter once the gauntlet
begins.

\begin{landscape}

\section{Endor Tournament}\label{endor-tournament}

The tournament is five consecutive solo battles. Alena can use items
between matches, and Roric, Vivian, and Sampson can each leave a Medical
Herb. If Alena needs healing and her inventory is full, consume an herb
before accepting or advancing past the replacement so it is not lost.

\begin{guidetable}

  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 8\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2832}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 8\tabcolsep) * \real{0.3746}}@{}}

1 & Hun & 70 & Straight physical offense. & Attack continuously and heal
only if Alena cannot safely absorb another hit. \\
2 & Roric & 90 & Attacks with a Boomerang and sometimes guards. & Keep
attacking through guarded rounds; use a herb afterward if needed. \\
3 & Vivian & 90 & Casts Firebal and Icebolt and can heal herself. &
Maintain pressure so her healing does not extend the fight; do not wait
too long to use a herb against spell damage. \\
4 & Sampson & 90 & Stronger physical attacks than the earlier opponents.
& Heal at a more conservative threshold and let the Iron Claw carry the
damage race. \\
5 & Linguar & 55 & Splits into one real body and three mirages each
round. & There is no reliable pattern in the supplied guides; choose a
target, endure misses, and heal based on HP rather than impatience. \\
\end{longtable}
}

\end{guidetable}
\end{landscape}
\chaptermark{Chapter 2: Alena's Journey to Endor}

At level 12 the sequence is possible; level 13 provides a safer HP and
damage margin. The Iron Claw is more important than squeezing in another
minor armor purchase. Alena's natural Agility and critical-hit rate can
shorten any round, but the route does not rely on a critical hit.

After Linguar falls, the announcer calls for the reigning champion,
Necrosaro. He does not appear, so Alena is declared the tournament
winner without a sixth fight. There is no achievement at the moment of
tournament victory; continue through the chapter-ending return sequence.

\section{The Empty Castle}\label{the-empty-castle}

As Alena leaves Endor Castle, a Santeem soldier delivers an urgent
warning and collapses. The supplied walkthroughs disagree on whether
Return can be cast during this sequence: Ramina routes the party back
with Return, while BTB and Dragon's Den report that the spell is
unavailable. Try Return if Brey has enough MP; if the game rejects it,
walk back through the travel-door route. Random encounters are
suppressed during the return trip, so no additional combat preparation
is required.

Enter Santeem Castle. The halls are empty. Inspect 1F, visit the throne
room on 2F, and climb to Alena's bedroom on 3F so Cristo and Brey's
comments establish that the entire court has vanished. Return to 1F and
leave through the front entrance. Reaching Santeem alone does not close
the chapter; exiting after the investigation does.

\begin{achievementalert}

\textbf{Achievement Alert: The Empty Castle}

\textbf{Trigger:} Investigate the deserted Santeem Castle after winning
the tournament, then leave through the front entrance to complete
Chapter 2.

\end{achievementalert}

\section{Chapter 2 Completion Check}\label{chapter-2-completion-check}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Achievements:} Sneaking Out, The Offering, The Kidnapped
  Imposter, Speak No Evil, and The Empty Castle unlocked.
\item
  \textbf{Party levels:} Approximately 12-13, with Brey at least level
  11 for Snowstorm.
\item
  \textbf{Alena:} Iron Claw and Feather Hat secured; the Iron Claw
  remains with her for Chapter 5 and cannot be purchased after the
  tournament.
\item
  \textbf{Cristo:} Half Plate Armor and Iron Shield preferred; Morning
  Star if the chapter budget allowed it.
\item
  \textbf{Brey:} Defensive equipment prioritized; Venomous Dagger is the
  preferred purchased weapon.
\item
  \textbf{Inventory:} Unused Strength, Agility, and Lifeforce seeds
  retained for Chapter 5 allocation.
\item
  \textbf{Carried forward:} Each character's levels and personal
  equipment, plus the separately tracked casino-coin balance.
\item
  \textbf{Not carried forward:} Loose Chapter 2 gold.
\item
  \textbf{Next chapter:} Taloon begins his career in Lakanaba.
\end{itemize}

\hyperref[table-of-contents]{Return to Table of Contents}

\chapter{Chapter 3: Taloon's Road to
Riches}\label{chapter-3-taloons-road-to-riches}

\section{Section Achievements: Chapter
3}\label{section-achievements-chapter-3}

}

\end{guidetable}

Chapter 3 follows Taloon, an employee in Lakanaba's weapon shop who
wants to own a store of his own. His route is built around commerce:
working for wages, recognizing valuable merchandise, selling armor where
demand is highest, recovering a rare statuette, and using his own shop
to turn inventory into the money needed for two major investments.

Taloon has no spells and begins far less combat-ready than Ragnar or
Alena. He compensates with high HP growth, broad equipment access,
unusually frequent monster drops during his chapter, and random special
actions in battle. Those actions cannot be selected directly, so the
safe route still depends on armor, Medical Herbs, and optional hired
companions.

The chapter's critical path is:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Work at Lakanaba's weapon shop, buy basic equipment, and learn about
  the Iron Safe.
\item
  Solve the Cave North of Lakanaba and leave with the Iron Safe.
\item
  Visit Foxville and Bonmalmo, free Grandpa Tom's son, and borrow his
  dog Tov.
\item
  Expose Foxville, free the architect Da Gardi, and reopen the bridge to
  Endor.
\item
  Deliver the Prince's Letter and Royal Scroll to prevent the war and
  earn permission to own a shop.
\item
  Recover and sell the Silver Statuette, then buy the vacant Endor shop
  for 35,000G.
\item
  Deliver seven Broad Swords and seven Half Plate Armors to the King for
  60,000G.
\item
  Give the 60,000G to the tunnel builder, wait for construction to
  finish, and cross to Branca.
\end{enumerate}

\section{Chapter 3 Economy Plan}\label{chapter-3-economy-plan}

Chapter 3 has three required cash thresholds:

\begin{guidetable}

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2662}}
  >{\raggedleft\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2399}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.4939}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
\color{GuideGreen}\bfseries Goal
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedleft

\midrule\noalign{}

Buy the Endor shop & 35,000G & Sell the Silver Statuette for 25,000G,
then raise the remaining 10,000G through Lakanaba wages, fixed treasure,
premium armor sales, and monster drops. \\
Fill the royal order & 22,400G purchase cost & Seven Broad Swords at
2,000G and seven Half Plate Armors at 1,200G; Neta's markup or repeated
loot sales fund the order. \\
Restart tunnel construction & 60,000G & The King pays exactly 60,000G
when the full equipment order is delivered. \\
\end{longtable}
}

\end{guidetable}

The optional Sword of Malice route is the fastest money engine but
depends on a customer offering one while Taloon works in Lakanaba. If
that happens, buy it into the employer's stock and never sell it to
another customer. Taloon can later purchase stocked copies for 3,500G
and give them to Neta, who resells merchandise at a large markup. If it
never appears, the chapter remains fully completable through fixed
treasure, ordinary equipment trading, and monster drops.

The Iron Safe prevents Taloon from losing gold after defeat during this
chapter. Obtain it before carrying serious money. It occupies one
inventory slot, so dungeon-loot trips still require inventory planning
even though the cash itself is protected.

\section{Lakanaba: Working Capital}\label{lakanaba-working-capital}

\textbf{Starting level:} 1\\
\textbf{Free recovery:} Sleep at Taloon's home\\
\textbf{Fixed pickup:} Medical Herb\\
\textbf{Repeatable errand:} Escort Grandpa Tom for 10G

Neta gives Taloon a \textbf{Lunch} before he leaves for work. It
restores HP and can replace one early Medical Herb, and she supplies
another after Taloon returns home for a new day. Go downstairs and
search the far-left pot for a hidden \textbf{Medical Herb}.

North of the house, Grandpa Tom asks to be taken to the House of
Healing. Walk into him to push him westward, steer him through the
entrance, and receive \textbf{10G}. Leaving and re-entering Lakanaba
resets this small errand, so it can provide emergency seed money, but
weapon-shop wages are much faster.

Speak to the innkeeper to hear about an Iron Safe in the cave north of
town. Then enter the weapon shop in the southwest and tell the owner
that Taloon is ready to work. Customers either buy the shop's goods or
offer equipment for the shop to purchase. Agree to ordinary sales
because Taloon's end-of-day commission rises with his sales volume.

The shop always stocks Clubs, Copper Swords, and Thorn Whips.
Boomerangs, Chain Sickles, and Swords of Malice appear only after a
customer sells one to the store. If a \textbf{Sword of Malice} appears,
accept it and refuse every later request to buy it; this preserves it in
shop stock for Taloon's own purchase later. Do not wait indefinitely for
the random offer.

Work roughly two days, or until Taloon can buy a Thorn Whip and basic
armor. The owner normally pays about 100G per completed shift. Sleep at
home for free, accept the next Lunch from Neta, and repeat only as long
as the guaranteed upgrades are useful.

Taloon begins in Basic Clothes and needs a weapon before field combat
becomes efficient. The practical departure set is Thorn Whip, Leather
Armor or Chain Mail, Leather Shield, Leather Hat, two Medical Herbs, and
the current Lunch. Reach approximately level 3 around Lakanaba, rest at
home, save, then travel northeast to the isolated cave on the northern
cape.

\section{Cave North of Lakanaba}\label{cave-north-of-lakanaba}

\textbf{Recommended level:} 3-4\\
\textbf{Fixed treasure:} Chain Sickle, Iron Safe\\
\textbf{Critical warning:} Bring a movable boulder into the Iron Safe
room before taking the chest.

Puzzle rooms in this cave do not generate random encounters, so take
time to position the boulders correctly. The monsters elsewhere are the
same low-tier enemies found around Lakanaba.

\subsection{Reservoir Route}\label{reservoir-route}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  From the entrance, walk west and descend the stairs.
\item
  Follow the linear passage until it reaches two stairways; both connect
  to the same route.
\item
  Examine the barrier holding back the first reservoir. The released
  water carries Taloon down to the next stairs, and this first mechanism
  resets when revisited.
\item
  Continue until reaching the second reservoir barrier.
\item
  Before opening it, walk west past the barrier and then north to the
  chest containing the \textbf{Chain Sickle}. Equip it immediately.
\item
  Return to the barrier and release the second reservoir. The water
  carries Taloon to the floor below; unlike the first mechanism, this
  change remains in place.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Rolling Boulder Puzzle}\label{rolling-boulder-puzzle}

From the landing, walk north and take the western branch, then descend
at its end. Ignore the nearby southern stairs and continue west to the
wall holding a large boulder. Stand below the barrier and walk south to
release it.

The boulder advances whenever Taloon moves. Keep it close enough to
follow the turns without letting it roll onto the wrong line. Move east
into the next corridor, south at the bend, then west at the first
available branch. Continue to the western wall and step north. The
boulder rolls south into the pit, creating a bridge. Cross it and
descend to the bottom floor.

If the boulder misses the pit, use the nearby stairs to reset the puzzle
room and try again. No treasure is lost.

\subsection{Iron Safe Trap}\label{iron-safe-trap}

Two small movable boulders are on the bottom floor. Use the leftmost
one.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Push the boulder north along the passage toward the room containing
  the gray chest.
\item
  Stop with the boulder inside the room and directly south of the chest
  before touching the treasure.
\item
  Open the chest and take the \textbf{Iron Safe}. Removing it raises a
  wall over the doorway and reveals a floor switch where the chest
  stood.
\item
  Push the boulder north onto that switch. The wall lowers and Taloon
  can leave.
\end{enumerate}

If Taloon takes the Iron Safe without bringing a boulder into the room,
he cannot reach a boulder after the wall closes. Do not save after
trapping him; reset and repeat the bottom-floor puzzle.

Retrace the cave route and step outside with the Iron Safe still in
Taloon's inventory.

\begin{achievementalert}

\textbf{Achievement Alert: It's a Trap}

\textbf{Trigger:} Take the Iron Safe, escape its sealed treasure room,
and leave the cave. The achievement description is ``Collect the Iron
Safe and leave.''

\end{achievementalert}

Return to Lakanaba, rest at home, and improve Taloon's armor if needed.
Raise him to approximately level 5 or 6 before continuing southeast into
the forest toward Foxville.

\section{Foxville: The False Village}\label{foxville-the-false-village}

\textbf{Recommended level:} 5-6\\
\textbf{Fixed treasure on first visit:} None\\
\textbf{Later reward:} Full Plate Armor

Travel east from Lakanaba, then turn south into the forest and enter the
isolated village. Follow the narrow northern approach into town. Speak
with everyone, especially the architect Da Gardi in the southeast house.
He is supposed to be repairing Bonmalmo's bridge but has become
distracted by his new life here.

Foxville's shop is part of the illusion. A listed Medical Herb produces
a Gum Pod, and the supposed 10G Broad Sword is only a Cypress Stick. Buy
neither. The looping exit and the villagers' repeated mannerisms are
further warnings that the town is not what it seems.

Enter the house behind the false shop and speak with the mayor. Accept
his offer to stay overnight. Taloon wakes alone in the forest with
Foxville gone. Continue southwest to Bonmalmo; the illusion cannot be
broken permanently until Taloon returns with a dog that hunts foxes.

\section{Bonmalmo: Prisoner, Prince, and
Bridge}\label{bonmalmo-prisoner-prince-and-bridge}

\textbf{Recommended level:} 5-6 on arrival; 9-10 before crossing to
Endor\\
\textbf{Inn:} 10G\\
\textbf{Required item:} Wing of Wyvern\\
\textbf{Story items:} Tov, Prince's Letter\\
\textbf{Reward from Foxville:} Full Plate Armor

Bonmalmo is preparing to invade Endor, but the broken southern bridge
has stalled its army. The King hired Da Gardi to repair it, connecting
the bridge problem to the architect trapped in Foxville.

Visit the castle and note three people:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The armor buyer in the southwest pays more than ordinary shops for
  armor.
\item
  Reed, the Prince of Bonmalmo, is in the northwest and asks Taloon to
  meet him behind the weapon shop at night.
\item
  Grandpa Tom's son is imprisoned in the basement cell east of the
  stairs.
\end{itemize}

Buy a \textbf{Wing of Wyvern} before entering the jail if Taloon does
not already carry one. The stairs to the jail are behind the throne
room. Guards patrol the basement; wait until they turn away, then move
to the eastern cell and talk through the bars. Give Grandpa Tom's son
the Wing of Wyvern so he can escape to Lakanaba. Being spotted only
resets Taloon's position, so watch the patrol pattern and try again
rather than rushing.

The Bonmalmo armor buyer is not a normal equipment shop. Save armor
drops and the coming Full Plate Armor for him rather than selling them
elsewhere.

\section{Tov and the Secret of
Foxville}\label{tov-and-the-secret-of-foxville}

Return north to Lakanaba. Grandpa Tom's son now stands near his father's
house and lets Taloon borrow \textbf{Tov}, his fox-hunting dog. Go
directly back to Foxville. Tov runs to the mayor's house and exposes the
mayor's true form.

Speak to the fox and let him go. He gives Taloon \textbf{Full Plate
Armor} in exchange for his freedom. Taloon cannot equip it, so reserve
it for Bonmalmo's premium armor buyer. With the illusion broken, Da
Gardi leaves to complete the bridge assignment.

Return to Lakanaba and speak with Grandpa Tom's son if Tov has not
already left Taloon, then travel back to Bonmalmo. Sell the Full Plate
Armor to the southwest buyer and speak with the King. Once Da Gardi's
return has been acknowledged, leave the castle and check the southern
route: the bridge to Endor is repaired.

\begin{achievementalert}

\textbf{Achievement Alert: The Architect}

\textbf{Trigger:} Expose Foxville with Tov, free Da Gardi, and speak
with the King of Bonmalmo so the bridge to Endor is repaired.

\end{achievementalert}

Before crossing, finish the Prince's request. Speak with Reed in the
castle during the day if he has not yet arranged the meeting. Wait until
night, walk behind the weapon shop on the east side of town, and accept
the \textbf{Prince's Letter}. It must be used while standing in front of
the Princess of Endor.

Use the repaired bridge as the next leveling boundary. South of
Bonmalmo, the encounter table becomes more demanding; bring Taloon
toward level 9 or 10 before committing to the repeated trips between
Endor and Bonmalmo.

\subsection{Foxville and Bonmalmo Region
Enemies}\label{foxville-and-bonmalmo-region-enemies}

\begin{guidetable}

Cross the repaired bridge and follow the river south to Endor.

\section{Endor: Preventing the War}\label{endor-preventing-the-war}

\textbf{Recommended level:} 9-10\\
\textbf{Inn:} 6G\\
\textbf{Story items:} Prince's Letter, Royal Scroll\\
\textbf{Major objective:} Permission to purchase a shop

Enter Endor Castle and stand directly in front of the Princess. Use the
\textbf{Prince's Letter} from Taloon's inventory rather than merely
talking to her. The King overhears the proposal and gives Taloon a
\textbf{Royal Scroll} for the King of Bonmalmo.

Return north across the repaired bridge, stand before the King of
Bonmalmo, and use the Royal Scroll. He abandons the invasion when he
realizes the marriage offers access to Endor's wealth without a war.
Travel back to Endor and speak to its King again. He rewards Taloon by
granting permission to operate a shop in the city.

The vacant shop is in the southwest corner of Endor. Speak with its
owner upstairs to learn the price: \textbf{35,000G}. The permission is
only one half of the requirement; Taloon still needs the purchase money.

In the northeast house, an art collector offers \textbf{25,000G} for the
Silver Statuette hidden in a cave northeast of Endor. That leaves a
10,000G funding gap. Before leaving, inspect the incomplete tunnel east
of Endor, then continue north to the actual statuette cave. The tunnel
is not the dungeon and cannot yet be crossed.

\subsection{Endor Equipment
Priorities}\label{endor-equipment-priorities}

The casino below Endor's inn is closed at this stage. Ragnar can be
found there, but he does not join Taloon and is not part of the chapter
route.

\section{Optional Hired Companions}\label{optional-hired-companions}

The Cave of the Silver Statuette can be cleared alone, but Endor offers
two temporary allies. Hire them immediately before leaving so their
five-day contracts cover the dungeon rather than routine errands.

Their fees are small compared with the 35,000G objective, and the extra
actions make both ordinary encounters and Metal Slime attempts safer.
Hire both for the first clear unless Taloon is already overleveled or
the player wants a solo challenge.

\section{Cave of the Silver
Statuette}\label{cave-of-the-silver-statuette}

\textbf{Recommended level:} 11\\
\textbf{Fixed treasure:} Wing of Wyvern, Morning Star, 760G, Medical
Herb, Iron Spear, Broad Sword, Half Plate Armor, Silver Statuette\\
\textbf{Required action:} Drain the upper reservoir to expose the lower
routes

Clear inventory space before entering. Taloon has only eight personal
item slots, and the Iron Safe plus equipped gear consume several of
them. The route identifies two valuable chests that can deliberately
remain unopened for Chapter 5.

\subsection{Entry and Drainage Switch}\label{entry-and-drainage-switch}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Follow the entrance passage until reaching stairs beside a boat.
  Ignore the stairs and board the boat.
\item
  Follow the waterway into the dark opening and fall with the boat to
  the lower flooded level.
\item
  Disembark on the northern landing. Take the western branch to the
  chest containing a \textbf{Wing of Wyvern}.
\item
  Return east, continue to the far wall, then turn south around the
  raised platform.
\item
  Take the stairs in the southeast corner to the reservoir-control
  floor.
\item
  Follow the northern route and turn west at the split. Continue north
  to the floor switch.
\item
  Stand on the switch and answer \textbf{Yes}. The water drains from
  this floor and floods the level below, opening both its boat route and
  the remaining treasure ledges.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Drained-Floor Treasure}\label{drained-floor-treasure}

Backtrack from the switch and collect:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Morning Star} from the chest near the western return route.
  Taloon cannot equip it, so reserve it for sale.
\item
  \textbf{760G} from the northern room.
\item
  \textbf{Medical Herb} from the far southern chest.
\item
  \textbf{Iron Spear} from the northeast chest.
\end{itemize}

The nearby western stairs provide the quickest exit to the cave entrance
after the statuette is secured.

\subsection{Flooded-Level Treasure and the
Statuette}\label{flooded-level-treasure-and-the-statuette}

Descend the newly exposed southeast stairs and board the waiting boat.
Sail north, turn west, pass the opening to the north, and continue south
into the isolated room containing a \textbf{Broad Sword}. The chest in
the northwest portion of the flooded level contains \textbf{Half Plate
Armor}.

The two adjacent chests in the top-right area of B3 are empty. They have
separate opened flags but contain no items, so the efficient route can
skip them.

Neither item is usable by Taloon. The long-term route leaves both chests
unopened so the Chapter 5 party can collect the +40 Broad Sword and +30
Half Plate Armor directly. If Taloon is still well short of 35,000G and
the player prefers less grinding now, collecting and selling them is
valid, but it exchanges future equipment for only 2,400G of normal
resale value.

Return to the boat and sail into the north-central room. Land on the
decorated platform, descend the stairs, and follow the final passage
north to the chest containing the \textbf{Silver Statuette}. Backtrack
to the drained-floor western stairs and leave the cave. A Wing of Wyvern
can shorten the return to Endor once Taloon is outside.

\subsection{Statuette Cave Enemies}\label{statuette-cave-enemies}

\section{Selling the Statuette and Buying the
Shop}\label{selling-the-statuette-and-buying-the-shop}

Return to the collector upstairs in Endor's northeast house and sell the
Silver Statuette for \textbf{25,000G}. Taloon now needs another 10,000G.

Use the safest available combination:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Sell the Full Plate Armor to Bonmalmo's premium buyer if it is still
  in Taloon's inventory.
\item
  Sell the cave's Morning Star. Equip the Iron Spear if Taloon lacks a
  Sword of Malice; otherwise sell it as well.
\item
  Sell ordinary monster drops and work additional Lakanaba shifts.
\item
  Revisit the statuette cave for battles and any treasure intentionally
  chosen for current funding rather than Chapter 5.
\end{enumerate}

The Iron Safe makes deliberate defeat a possible return shortcut without
losing gold, but it is not required and still leaves Taloon needing
revival. A Wing of Wyvern is cleaner when available.

Once Taloon holds at least 35,000G, go to the vacant shop in southwest
Endor, climb upstairs, and pay the owner. Neta and the couple's child
relocate from Lakanaba, and Neta begins operating the new store.

\begin{achievementalert}

\textbf{Achievement Alert: Moving In}

\textbf{Trigger:} Pay 35,000G for the vacant Endor shop and complete the
family move. The achievement description is ``Purchase the shop in
Endor.''

\end{achievementalert}

Speaking with Neta from behind the counter now provides free rest. She
also accepts merchandise, sells it during the next business day at a
markup, and gives Taloon the proceeds. This is the point where the
chapter's scalable money engine becomes available.

\begin{landscape}

\section{The Royal Equipment Order}\label{the-royal-equipment-order}

Return to Endor Castle and speak with the King after buying the shop. He
orders \textbf{seven Broad Swords and seven Half Plate Armors} for the
royal guard and promises 60,000G when the order is complete. Deliver
equipment to the guard in the small northeast room on 1F; he accepts
partial shipments and reports how many pieces remain.
